## 10. Multi-Line Function-Head Resolution in `resolve_symbol`

[`scripts/todo-graph/resolve_symbol.py`](../../scripts/todo-graph/resolve_symbol.py) locates a C function definition by matching a head regex against ONE line at a time: it requires the return-type tokens and the symbol to sit on the same physical line. Every definition whose head spans two lines is therefore invisible to it -- and `resolve_symbol` is what [`scripts/lint/check_stub_behind_stamp.py`](../../scripts/lint/check_stub_behind_stamp.py) (lint Check 7) calls to decide whether a `[x]`-stamped symbol is really a `return CONSTANT;` stub. A symbol the resolver cannot find is not reported as unverifiable; it is simply not checked, so the blind spot is silent by construction and reads as a clean lint.

This section owns ONLY that head-matching gap -- the PARSE. It does NOT re-cover §9's `stamped_items` cache shape, Check 7's `lint.sh` wiring, the repo-escape guard, or the eight regression sub-tests §9 shipped; all of those are live and stay untouched. It also does not change what Check 7 REPORTS: making an unresolvable symbol countable instead of silent is §11's job, split out of this section on the split predictor's verdict before any code was written, because it changes what a clean lint MEANS and carries a different risk (a noisy gate) than a parse fix does.

> [!NOTE]
> **Why it was parked and why it is open again.** §9 filed this as a known limitation on 2026-05-02 with two candidate fixes: a sliding-window head join, or delegation to `mcp__lsp-bridge__definition` "once TODO-07 lsp-bridge is generally available". TODO-07 has since shipped that tool, so the stated blocker cleared -- but §9 carries Verified + Quality-reviewed stamps, where a bare `- [ ]` is invisible to the triage oracle. Reopening it as its own section is the shape CLAUDE.md prescribes for residue in a stamped section, and it is a reopen rather than a duplicate: §9's item is `- [/]` pointing here, and this section points back.

**Decide the approach before writing code -- the two candidates are not equivalent.** The sliding-window join keeps the resolver stdlib-only, in-process and fork-free, which is what lets `lint.sh` call it per-ref at commit time. Delegating to lsp-bridge buys real parsing but makes a commit-time lint depend on a language server being installed, warm, and answering; measure that before adopting it, and prefer the local parse if the accuracy delta is small.

- [x] ADD a windowed join as a FALLBACK pass instead of replacing the line-by-line head match, so pass 1's resolutions are never displaced and the resolved count cannot fall
      Pass 2 runs only when pass 1 returns None, joins up to 2 preceding type-only lines with the terminal line, and confirms through the same walk. The original wording said "Replace" -- which is exactly what the reverted attempt did.
- [x] Cover the shapes the current regex drops: return type on its own line, `static inline` split across lines, `__attribute__((...))`-prefixed definitions, and a pointer return type whose `*` binds to either line
      `_DECL_MODIFIER_RE` handles the attribute / calling-convention prefix. MEASURED: zero live definitions use that shape today (`EFIAPI` appears only in typedefs, `src/boot/uefi/efi.h:173`), so that arm is forward-looking robustness, not a coverage win.
- [x] Keep the false-positive bar §9 set: a prototype must still walk past to the real definition, and a call site or macro invocation must not be read as a head
      The confirmation walk is now paren-depth aware, which also fixes two latent bugs in the SINGLE-LINE path: a `struct` defined inside the parameter list had its brace taken as the body, and `TYPE\nSYMBOL(args) = {...};` was accepted because `=` was ignored.
- [x] Measure lsp-bridge delegation against the local parse -- **verdict: NOT rejected on accuracy or speed; deferred as operator-gated on the dependency question**
      `textDocument/documentSymbol` returned body ranges identical to the local parse on both newly-resolved refs, also resolved `kmalloc` at 666:694 which the local parse cannot, cost 0.10s cold init + 0.02-0.10s per file, and worked with NO compile database. Details in Notes.
- [x] Regression fixtures in [`scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh`](../../scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh) for every shape above, each mutation-checked
      Sub-tests 14j-14s assert EXACT `(start, end)` tuples, not "a finding appeared". 14j-14n fail when pass 2 is disabled, 14q when depth-awareness is removed, 14p when the depth-0 reject path is removed, 14s when pass 1 is displaced; 14o is labelled in-file as a behavior pin no single mutation flips.
- [x] Gate the change on the live corpus, not on fixtures -- [`corpus_resolution_snapshot.py`](../../scripts/todo-graph/corpus_resolution_snapshot.py) pins each ref's exact `(path, start, end)` and fails on a lost or MOVED mapping
      Added because the coverage floor records a COUNT, and a count cannot see a wrong new resolution offsetting a lost one. Result: 0 lost, 0 moved, 2 added, both ground-truthed by hand. Baseline raised 55 -> 57.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: resolve multi-line function heads so Check 7 stops skipping them"`

**Test checkpoint:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` green with the new fixtures; `bash scripts/lint.sh` Check 7 still reports zero findings on the live tree (the fix widens what is CHECKED, so a new finding here is a real stub-behind-stamp and must be triaged, not suppressed); `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green in aggregate.

> **Notes:**
> - Two-pass `resolve_symbol()`: pass 1 is the unchanged single-line scan, pass 2 joins type-only lines only when pass 1 fails, so coverage cannot drop by construction.
> - Coverage 55 -> 57, gated by `corpus_resolution_snapshot.py` (0 lost, 0 moved, 2 added) rather than by the count; a corpus sweep now finds zero split-head definitions still unresolved, closing this class.
> - That gate keys PER OCCURRENCE and fails CLOSED (exit 3) on an empty, legacy, stale or malformed cache -- both holes were found by review, and a gate that fails open is worse than no gate.
> - The depth-aware walk also fixed two latent SINGLE-LINE bugs (parameter-scope struct brace read as a body; `= {...}` initializer accepted as a definition) -- which is why the snapshot, not the count, was the acceptance test.
> - lsp-bridge verdict, MEASURED: `documentSymbol` matched the local parse wherever both resolve, also resolved `kmalloc`, cost ~0.1s, and needed no compile database -- the premise that it loses on accuracy or speed did NOT hold.
> - Delegation is deferred as an operator-gated DEPENDENCY call (commit-time lint gaining a hard `clangd` requirement), not closed as rejected; the local parse ships meanwhile because it adds no dependency.
> - Cost: the fallback pass first cost 7.1x on the Check 7 walk (+420ms per commit); after a substring pre-filter it is +1.6ms/+1.9% on this host and +13ms/+17% on the reviewer's -- single-digit-to-low-double-digit ms either way, both recorded rather than picking the flattering one.
> - Scope boundary: this section owns the PARSE only. The largest remaining resolvable class is 35 refs defined past `_DEF_HEAD_LIMIT` (512 lines), which is coverage and belongs to §12 (split out of §11 with the rest of the resolver-accuracy work).

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` -- expect `144/144 passed, 0 failed` (sub-tests 14j-14z cover the split-head shapes, the negatives, and the corpus gate). No kernel test surface: this is host tooling, so there is no `TEST_CAT_*` category or bat.
> **Verified:** 2026-08-06 | commit `cc05f2e9` + review fixes | 7/7 items | build OK | coverage 55 -> 57, corpus gate 0 lost / 0 moved / 2 added
> **Deferred:** [M] resolver cache is line-bounded not byte-bounded, keys on path alone, and the fixed pre-slice shortens the body window near the head limit -- all pre-existing -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-06-todo-metadata-layer.md §12` (item: "Bound the resolver by BYTES and detect input mutation" at line 500)
> **Deferred:** [M] backslash-newline splicing is mis-lexed (C splices before lexing); raised as a regression but reproduces identically on the pre-§10 resolver -> XREF: `00-infrastructure/TODO-06-todo-metadata-layer.md §12` (item: "Handle backslash-newline splicing in the resolver lexer" at line 502)

## 11. Count What Check 7 Cannot Resolve Instead of Skipping It

Check 7 ([`scripts/lint/check_stub_behind_stamp.py`](../../scripts/lint/check_stub_behind_stamp.py)) walks every `kind=symbol` ref with a paired file, calls `resolve_symbol`, and classifies the body. A ref the resolver CANNOT locate produces no output at all: it is not a finding, not a warning, and not a count. So "Check 7 clean" today conflates two very different states -- every stamped symbol was found and none is a stub, versus some stamped symbols were never examined. §9's `Verified` stamp records "zero stub-behind-stamp findings on the current 227 TODOs" against exactly that ambiguity.

This section makes the second state countable. It is the REPORTING half of the resolver work; §10 owns the parse itself and is a prerequisite, because widening what resolves shrinks this number and the two changes would otherwise fight over the same baseline.

**The failure mode to design against is a noisy gate, not a quiet one.** Check 7 runs in the pre-commit lint, so an unresolved-symbol count that is nonzero on the live tree and printed as an ERROR would block every commit in the repo on day one. Establish the live-tree number FIRST, then choose the severity that number justifies -- a WARN carrying the count is the likely answer, with an ERROR reserved for a regression above the recorded baseline. Do not pick the severity before measuring.

- [x] Separate "resolved, not a stub" from "symbol not found" in `check_stub_behind_stamp.py`, and emit the unresolved count rather than dropping those refs silently
      `_walk` returns `(findings, resolved, unresolved)`; a `resolved is None` ref is recorded rather than `continue`d. NOTE `is_stub_body` returning None means NOT A STUB -- the healthy case -- so the only genuine silent skip was the unresolved one.
- [x] Measure the live-tree count and record it -- **55 resolved of 189 refs; 134 unresolved (71% blind)**
      The BEFORE/AFTER framing this item was written with no longer applies: §10's first rewrite was REVERTED for taking coverage to 1/189 (commit `5cff59cc`). 55 was the pre-§10 baseline; §10's landed two-pass resolver raised it to **57**, which is the current floor.
- [x] Choose the severity from that measurement -- **WARN on the standing gap, ERROR on a REGRESSION against a recorded baseline**
      Both were live options and the measurement decided it. ERROR on the standing 134 would block every commit in the repo until it reached zero, which is why Check 24 warns rather than blocks. But a DROP is categorically different: an unresolved symbol produces no finding, so losing resolution looks exactly like passing. `scripts/lint/stub-lint-baseline.json` records the floor (55 when this shipped, raised to 57 by §10); `rc 7` on a drop. **Proven against the real failure: crippling the resolver reports `resolved 0 < baseline` and exits 7.** This gate would have caught §10's first regression in one line at commit time, where a green 115/115 suite did not. §10 showed its limit too: a count cannot see a MOVED mapping, which is why `corpus_resolution_snapshot.py` now sits beside it.
- [x] Classify the 134 so the next step targets the real cause rather than the assumed one
      **78 (58.2%) DECLARATION-only** -- the ref names a header that declares the symbol while the body lives in a `.c`. **46 (34.3%) comment-only or absent** -- the symbol appears in that file solely inside comments, or not at all. **10 (7.5%) genuine parser gap.** This reframes §10: multi-line head parsing addresses class C, which is 7.5% of the gap, while the dominant cause is a ref pointing at the wrong file.
- [x] Report one counting base -- every occurrence now lands in exactly one bucket, and the buckets sum to the population or the check refuses to publish a ratio
      `_walk` returns a bucket dict instead of `(resolved_n, sorted(set(unresolved)))`; `main` asserts `resolved + sum(buckets) == occurrences` and returns rc 6 on a mismatch rather than printing a plausible number. Dedup survives only on the human sample list, where collapsing repeats is readability, not measurement. The floor still compares `resolved_n` against itself, so gate semantics did not move.
- [x] Decide what a class-A ref MEANS -- **decided: the bucket names describe EVIDENCE, never cause, because no lexical test can separate the two**
      Hand-sampled as the item asked. A causal label is unsafe: `src/kernel/test/test_alpc.c` only CALLS `kmalloc_fail_next` (defined `src/kernel/mm/heap.c:499`) and `src/kernel/ipc/alpc.c:23` only calls `alpc_port_init` (defined `alpc_port.c:284`), so a call site carries `symbol(` while no file-local resolver can ever fix it. Shipped `no_calllike_token` / `unresolved_calllike` instead of ABSENT/UNRESOLVED. Assigning cause needs cross-file definition evidence -> §12.
- [x] Count every `kind=symbol` occurrence, not only the ones that survive validation -- **the denominator was fail-open, twice**
      Design review found the first layer: 503 paired refs existed but only 193 reached resolution (246 nonexistent file, 63 non-C suffix, 1 repo-escape), all dropped before counting, so retargeting a ref at a nonexistent path made the ratio look BETTER. The adversarial review then found the SAME defect one level up in my own fix -- `occurrences` was incremented after the file/symbol pairing check, hiding 1,122 refs that name a symbol with no file. Population is now the full 1,625 `kind=symbol` set; every drop is a bucket.
- [x] Make the population invariant cover omissions BEFORE `_walk`'s classification, not just branches after it
      The fixture that guards this had to be strengthened after mutation testing: asserting an `unpaired-ref=` key exists is not enough, because reintroducing the bug leaves the key present at 0 and the weak form passed. It now derives the expected count independently from the cache and asserts equality, which fails at `503 != 1625` under the mutation.
- [x] Surface the coverage line and the baseline bypass through `lint.sh`, not just on the helper's stderr
      The helper writes coverage to stderr because stdout is its findings channel, and `lint.sh`'s rc-0 branch read stdout ONLY -- so a normal lint run published no ratio at all and `STUB_LINT_ALLOW_NO_BASELINE=1` produced none of the promised visible warning. Coverage is now an `info` line and the bypass a counted WARNING. Pinned by an INTEGRATION fixture that runs `lint.sh` itself, since a helper-only test could not have caught it.
- [x] Fail CLOSED when the baseline itself is missing or malformed -- **REVERSES this section's own earlier warn-only decision**
      The earlier shape swallowed every read/parse error into `baseline = None`, which skipped the floor and still exited 0. It protected a bootstrap case that cannot occur: `stub-lint-baseline.json` is TRACKED, so a fresh clone always has it. What warn-only actually bought was a silent unrecorded bypass. Now rc 8 (wired in `lint.sh`), with `STUB_LINT_ALLOW_NO_BASELINE=1` as the explicit, self-announcing skip that synthetic-tree fixtures use.
- [/] Decide the lsp-bridge delegation question -- **operator-gated: it is a dependency call, not an accuracy one**
      §10 measured `textDocument/documentSymbol` returning body ranges identical to the local parse where both resolve, additionally resolving `kmalloc` at 666:694, at ~0.1s cold init and with NO compile database. So delegation is not rejected on merit; what it costs is a hard `clangd` dependency for the pre-commit lint, which is a stop-and-ask class change. **Parked operator-gated:** an unattended run may not add a dependency to the commit-time lint, and no TODO owns that call -- only the operator can take it. -> XREF: `TODO-06 §10` Notes.
- [x] Regression fixtures: coverage counting, the bucket invariant, the baseline gate, and a structural perf guard -- each mutation-checked
      `scripts/overnight/tests/test_stub_lint_coverage.py` (17 tests): buckets sum to the population, the denominator equals the cache's own `kind=symbol` count, missing-file refs are counted, bucket names carry no causal claim, a drop below baseline exits 7, a corrupt / non-integer / non-object / invalid-UTF-8 baseline exits 8, the bypass announces itself, `lint.sh` surfaces the ratio at WARN severity, and the classifier stays cached per FILE. Plus `test_build.sh` sub-test 14b2. MUTATIONS VERIFIED: bare `continue` for `missing_file` trips the invariant (257 != 503); counting after the pairing check fails the population test (503 != 1625); `baseline = None` instead of rc 8 fails fail-closed; `absent=` fails the no-causal-claim test; the narrow `(OSError, JSONDecodeError)` tuple fails the UTF-8 test; removing the per-file cache fails the perf guard; downgrading WARN to info fails the severity test.
- [x] Do not let a regression test be able to wedge the gate it protects
      The mutation fixtures rewrote the TRACKED `stub-lint-baseline.json` in place and restored it in a `finally` -- which does not run on SIGKILL, on an external timeout, or on a crash mid-write, and two concurrent runs interleave. Any of those leaves the repo floor corrupt (blocking every later commit) or artificially low (silently weakening the gate). Added a `STUB_LINT_BASELINE` path override; every mutation now runs against a tempfile, and one fixture asserts the tracked file is byte-identical after the whole battery.
- [x] Keep the pre-commit cost honest -- **the first cut regressed Check 7 by 5.62x and that is not shippable**
      Classifying each unresolved ref with its own full-file scan took the check from 147.4ms to 828.6ms; it runs in the pre-commit hook for every contributor on every commit, so that is ~0.7s added to every commit in the repo. Fixed by caching the comment/string-stripped text per FILE (it does not depend on the symbol), replacing the Python character loop with one C-level regex, and pre-filtering on a cached raw-text substring test. **Final: 152.0ms -> 253.4ms, 1.67x / +101ms**, with the bucket counts byte-identical at every step. The residue buys classifying 136 refs that were previously dropped and counting 1,625 instead of 189.
- [x] Commit: `"lint: make Check 7 count the symbols it cannot resolve"`

**Test checkpoint:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` green with the new fixtures; `bash scripts/lint.sh` completes with the unresolved count reported and zero NEW errors on the live tree; `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` green in aggregate.

> **Test runner:** `python3 scripts/overnight/tests/test_stub_lint_coverage.py` -- expect `PASS: Check 7 coverage counting + regression floor + bucket invariant` (17 tests); plus `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` at `146/146 passed`. No kernel test surface: this is host tooling, so there is no `TEST_CAT_*` category or bat.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped: `_walk` returns a seven-way bucket dict instead of `(resolved_n, deduped_list)`, and `main` refuses to publish a ratio unless the buckets sum to the occurrence population (rc 6).
> - The denominator was fail-open at TWO levels -- 503 paired refs counted as 189, then the first fix hid a further 1,122 unpaired refs behind the pairing check. Population is now the full 1,625 `kind=symbol` set.
> - Bucket names state evidence, not cause -- a call site carries `symbol(` while the definition lives in another file, so ABSENT/UNRESOLVED would have mislabelled a large share of both.
> - Baseline handling reversed to fail-CLOSED (rc 8, wired in `lint.sh`) because the file is tracked; `STUB_LINT_ALLOW_NO_BASELINE=1` is the explicit self-announcing bypass, and `STUB_LINT_BASELINE` keeps mutation fixtures off tracked state.
> - Cost held to +101ms (1.67x) after the first cut regressed this pre-commit check 5.62x: strip once per FILE, one C-level regex, cached raw-text pre-filter.
> - Coverage did not move (57 resolved, floor unchanged) -- this section changed what is REPORTED; §12 owns moving the number and now also owns wiring the identity snapshot into a gate.

## 12. Resolver Coverage Past the Head Limit, and Lexer/Cache Robustness

> **Spawned-by:** §11 (split)

§10 fixed the multi-line head PARSE and §11 made the remaining blind spot COUNTABLE. What is left is the resolver's own accuracy gap: 35 refs whose definitions sit past `_DEF_HEAD_LIMIT`, a declaration-following class whose recorded size does not reproduce, and two robustness limits §10's review surfaced and deferred rather than fixed under a parse-only scope. These are one concern -- what [`resolve_symbol`](../../scripts/todo-graph/resolve_symbol.py) can find, and how safely it reads a file to find it.

This section was split out of §11 on the split predictor's `SPLIT-RECOMMENDED` verdict before any code was written -- the same way §11 was split out of §10, and for the same reason: §11's charter is Check 7's REPORTING contract (a noisy-gate risk), while widening resolution is a correctness risk against a live corpus. Mixing them puts two different failure modes behind one review.

It does NOT re-cover §10's two-pass head match, its paren-depth-aware confirmation walk, the `corpus_resolution_snapshot.py` gate, or §11's counting base and baseline machinery. All of those are live and stay untouched.

**The corpus gate is the acceptance test, not the count.** §10 proved a count cannot see a MOVED mapping -- a wrong new resolution offsets a lost one and the floor still passes. Every item here lands with `corpus_resolution_snapshot.py` reporting 0 lost / 0 moved, and every ADDED mapping ground-truthed by hand before the baseline is raised.

**Work the classes in measured size order, which is NOT the order they were discovered in.** §11's population repair changed the picture completely: against the true population of 1,625 occurrences the buckets are unpaired-ref 1122, missing-file 246, calllike-unresolved 89, non-C-suffix 63, no-calllike-token 47, path-escape 1. The two largest classes are not parse gaps -- together 84% of the population -- so the cheapest large wins are upstream of the resolver's definition rule entirely. Those two are §13's charter, not this section's. (§13 shipped, and its own measurement disproved the plan recorded here: they are NOT repaired in `build.py` extraction and the cache is unchanged, because a basename is a fact about the source tree and the cache's freshness check cannot invalidate one. Both repairs apply at RESOLUTION time, in a shared rule that decides which FILE this section's definition rule is pointed at.) This section owns the resolver, so its largest class is the 35 head-limit refs.

- [/] Wire `corpus_resolution_snapshot.py` into a GATE -- a scalar floor cannot see a lost identity that another occurrence offsets
      - Decided NOT to auto-wire this section, after two Codex design rounds surfaced real correctness gaps in every mechanism tried.
      - A pre-commit hook conditioned on the worktree diff validates the WRONG artifact under partial staging, and never fires at all in a clean CI checkout with no base-SHA wired.
      - Occurrence keys (`item_idx`/`ref_i`) are stable only while the cache itself is held fixed, so a naive tracked-baseline WARN would either miss real regressions (downgraded to WARN) or fire on ordinary TODO edits (kept as ERROR).
      - The identity mechanism itself is unaffected and still works correctly on demand -- `write`/`compare` proved 0 lost / 0 moved across every mechanism this section shipped.
      - Blocked on: a real automated wiring needs either index-blob materialization at pre-commit (candidate resolver run against the STAGED blob, not the worktree file) or a dedicated CI job comparing against the push's base SHA -- both are scoped engineering beyond this section, not a quick gate.
      -> XREF: `TODO-06 §11` -- §11 established the reporting contract this gate would enforce.
      -> XREF: `TODO-06 §16` -- the OWNER of the automated wiring, filed 2026-08-06 (item: "Choose the wiring mechanism on the two failure modes named here"). This park was previously recorded as "unfiled pending a session", which left it with no owner able to re-open it.
- [x] Resolve definitions sitting past `_DEF_HEAD_LIMIT` (512 lines) -- **35 refs, and the single largest resolvable class**, ahead of class B
      - `_DEF_HEAD_LIMIT` removed; the head search is now positionally unbounded, bounded instead by a new `_MAX_FILE_BYTES` (16 MiB) ceiling on the INPUT that raises `ResolverInputError` rather than silently truncating.
      - Candidate discovery uses a per-file lexical index (`_FileIndex`, one regex pass for line-start offsets + non-code spans) instead of a per-line Python scan, keeping the unbounded search affordable (1.39x vs. a naive 2.63x).
      - Measured: +35 resolved, 0 lost / 0 moved (`corpus_resolution_snapshot.py`), all 35 hand-verified as real single-line function definitions -- `kmalloc` at `src/kernel/mm/heap.c:666`, `schedule` at `src/kernel/sched/task.c:1721`, and 33 others across boot/kernel/desktop sources.
- [x] Follow a DECLARATION to its definition (class B) -- re-measured against the resolver's own decl rule: **20, not 78 or 19**
      - The recorded 78 was a looser probe; re-running with `_confirm_definition`'s own "decl" verdict against the CURRENT (head-limit-widened) resolver gives 20 -- one more than the 19 an earlier draft measured, since widening the head search itself changed what counts as decl-only.
      - New `follow_declaration(file_path, symbol, repo_root)` in `resolve_symbol.py`: when the only candidate in a HEADER classifies "decl" (no local body), follow to the repo's own documented `include/` mirrors `src/` convention (CLAUDE.md "Repository Layout") -- a basename-unique `.c` sibling, rejected if the matched definition is `static` (internal linkage cannot satisfy an external declaration).
      - Deliberately NOT a general cross-file symbol search (Codex design review: a bare unique-name search can bind an unrelated same-named definition in a different link product with no way to prove linkage) -- restricting to the header's own basename-unique sibling sidesteps that.
      - Measured: 18/20 resolve this way (all header-declared, all non-static, hand-verified); the remaining 2 are `extern` declarations inside a `.c` file (`src/kernel/test/test_runner.c` -> `test_boot_info.c`/`test_kernel_config.c`) which the convention does not cover and are left unresolved rather than guessed at.
      - Wired into `check_stub_behind_stamp.py`'s walk as a fallback when direct resolution fails; a followed definition reports findings at the FILE THAT HAS THE BODY, not the declaring header. The identity snapshot (`corpus_resolution_snapshot.collect()`) tries this same fallback, so its 0-lost/0-moved proof genuinely covers all 54 added mappings, not only the 36 from direct resolution (Codex adversarial: the first pass omitted this and the proof was incomplete).
      - **Accepted scope boundary:** basename uniqueness is checked tree-WIDE (`root.glob("src/**/<basename>.c")`), not scoped to the header's own link product (kernel vs. user-program vs. test vs. host-tool) -- a header could in principle bind to an unrelated same-named `.c` in a different subsystem. All 18 shipped mappings were individually hand-verified as correct; a true fix needs an explicit header-to-implementation mapping this repo does not currently expose, tracked as future work rather than built under this section's time budget.
      - **Fixed before shipping (Codex adversarial, 4 rounds):** the internal-linkage guard first only checked for a LEADING `static` token on the symbol's own head line (missed `inline static int foo(void)`); widened to check `static` anywhere on the masked head line, but that STILL missed `static` sitting ALONE on the line before an otherwise self-sufficient single-line head (a real repo idiom: `src/libs/miniz/miniz.c:4482-4483`), because `resolve_symbol` resolves that shape via pass 1 with `res[1]` pointing at the symbol's own line, never the `static` line. Fixed by walking back through preceding declaration-specifier lines.
      - Round 2 of that fix appended each preceding line before checking whether it was type-only, so the first disqualifying line still got searched for `static`. Confirmed live false rejection: `src/libs/monocypher/monocypher.c:158` is an unrelated one-line `static u64 x64(...)` immediately before line 159's real, external-linkage `crypto_verify16` -- fixed by checking type-only BEFORE appending, not after.
      - Round 3: the walk was bounded by `_WINDOW_LINES` (3 lines) -- the split-head resolver's OWN unrelated contract, not a real limit on how many lines a valid C declaration-specifier sequence can span (`static\ninline\nconst\nint foo(void)` is valid C, `static` 4 lines back). A BLANK line also incorrectly terminated the walk (`_TYPE_ONLY_LINE_RE` requires >= 1 character). Fixed with a new, generous `_LINKAGE_LOOKBACK_LIMIT` (32 lines, a pathological-input bound only) and blank lines now treated the same as type-only ones.
      - Round 4: a preprocessor-CONDITIONAL `static` (`#if.../static/#endif`, real and live at `src/libs/mbedtls/library/sha512.c:558-566`) is undecidable without preprocessing -- the walk now fails closed on `#if`/`#ifdef`/`#ifndef`/`#else`/`#elif`/`#endif` specifically. A first draft over-broadly failed closed on ANY `#`-prefixed line, live-regressing `test_desktop_reset` (preceded by a plain `#include`, not conditional) from resolved to unresolved -- caught via this section's own corpus-snapshot re-verification (111 -> 110) before it shipped, narrowed to the conditional-directive family only. A macro that EXPANDS to `static` (`#define STBIWDEF static`, real in vendored `stb_image.h`) stays an accepted scope boundary: undetectable without real preprocessing, and none of the 18 shipped mappings are affected.
      - Round 5: `_mask_line_comments` (per-physical-line only) could not see a block comment whose CLOSE shares a line with the directive it hides (`/* explanation\n */ #endif`), reading raw `*/ #endif` text as neither type-only nor a directive, so it was wrongly treated as a confident non-static boundary. Fixed by reusing `_FileIndex`'s already-computed, multi-line-aware span data (`candidate_lines`'s own mechanism) to blank comments across the WHOLE file once (`_masked_text_for_index`), so a line ending mid-comment reads correctly regardless of how many physical lines the comment spans.
      - Round 6: a standalone `__attribute__((...))`/`[[...]]` line between `static` and an otherwise self-sufficient head (`static\n__attribute__((noinline))\nvoid foo(void)`, valid real GCC style) terminated the lookback before reaching `static`. Rather than enumerate one more C/GCC declaration-specifier shape, the DEFAULT flipped: the walk now recognizes a small set of known-safe "continue" patterns (blank, type-only, standalone attribute) plus a small set of CONFIDENT boundaries (a masked line ending `;`/`}`, or a non-conditional directive), and FAILS CLOSED on anything matching neither -- closing the whole class of "next unenumerated shape," not just this one instance.
      - Own-motion, same mechanism: `is_stub_body`'s per-line comment stripper had the SAME multi-line-comment blindness, silently missing a real stub-behind-stamp whose body contained a multi-line comment (`fb_get_output_count()` in `src/kernel/drivers/framebuffer.c`, surfaced once this section's coverage work reached it) -- fixed by reusing the same `_masked_text_for_index` mechanism; `fb_get_output_count` marked `INTENTIONAL-STUB` (genuine, documented single-output placeholder).
      - Own-motion, `is_stub_body` again: a genuine TWO-physical-line body (`int f(void) {` / `    return 0; }`) was misread as a one-liner (slice-emptiness cannot distinguish "one physical line" from "opener+closer on adjacent lines"), searched for `}` only on the OPENER's line, and silently missed a real stub. Fixed by finding the matching closer's exact `(line, col)` via the same depth-aware scan used elsewhere, regardless of physical-line span.
      - Own-motion, `is_stub_body` a third time: the `INTENTIONAL-STUB` allowlist honored a bare SUBSTRING match, so negated prose (`/* not an INTENTIONAL-STUB */`), a bare token with no reason, and a string-literal occurrence all silently suppressed a real finding -- fail-OPEN on the suppression path, the worse direction. Fixed with a regex requiring the documented `/* INTENTIONAL-STUB: <reason> */` shape, tolerating a reason that wraps onto later physical lines (both real markers in this tree do). Caught before shipping by re-testing this section's OWN two markers (`hpet_available`, `fb_get_output_count`) against the FIRST tightened regex draft, which required the closer `*/` on the SAME line and broke both.
      - **Rejected (Codex adversarial), converging the review after 10 rounds:** binding the marker regex to a real lexical block-comment span (vs. a raw-line text search) so a marker-shaped string INSIDE an unrelated later statement on the SAME physical line cannot suppress a finding. Verified this shape has ZERO precedent anywhere in this repository (`grep` for a one-line function body followed by any trailing statement: no matches) and Codex's own finding explicitly introduces it as "for example" -- unlike every prior fix in this section (all cited a real, live file:line), this is a purely adversarially-constructed shape with no corpus grounding. Ten review rounds converged on real, corpus-verified fixes each time until this one; continuing to chase a hypothetical with no precedent is no longer proportionate.
      - **Rejected, reiterated (Codex adversarial):** the SAME macro-expands-to-`static` class already documented above, re-raised with a new example (`#define LZ4_FORCE_INLINE static inline __attribute__((always_inline))` in vendored `src/libs/lz4/lz4.c`) and a stronger ask: fail closed whenever ANY unrecognized identifier appears near the declaration, including the symbol's own head line. Rejected: kernel/boot C is saturated with legitimate ALL-CAPS type/macro tokens (`UINT64`, `EFI_STATUS`, `BOOLEAN`, ...) with no storage-class effect; a blanket "unrecognized identifier -> fail closed" heuristic would gut most of this section's own resolution gains, not just close the LZ4 gap. Same accepted scope boundary as the STBIWDEF/MBEDTLS_STATIC_TESTABLE case.
      - **Rejected (Codex adversarial, with reasoning):** hardening the file-content cache against a same-size, `os.utime`-forged-timestamp rewrite landing MID-lint-run. This requires a coordinated adversarial process actively forging file metadata while THIS SINGLE lint invocation is running -- outside this tool's threat model (a local pre-commit hook over a git working tree; the module's OWN docstring already states "safe today only because each lint invocation is a fresh interpreter" as the accepted baseline). Content-hashing every read to close this gap would add real per-commit overhead to defend against an adversary this tool was never designed to survive.
      - **Rejected (Codex adversarial, with reasoning):** escalating an ambiguous-escape-splice hit to a blocking Check 7 infrastructure error, rather than the current "resolves to unresolved, same as any other unresolved-ref reason." An unresolved ref already produces no finding for dozens of OTHER reasons in this exact lint check (decl-only, missing-file, no-calllike-token) -- all deliberately WARN-not-ERROR by this TODO's own §11 design (an unresolved ref is a standing, tracked gap, not a per-commit blocker). Making THIS ONE unresolved-reason blocking would be inconsistent with that design and disproportionate to a corpus-verified single hit that sits inside a comment, not live code. It is also strictly SAFER than the pre-fix behavior, which silently returned a WRONG range rather than no range at all.
- [x] Bound the resolver by BYTES and detect input mutation -- the file cache is line-bounded only, and its key is the path alone
      - `_read_file_lines` now opens the file once, `fstat`s the descriptor BEFORE and after the read (immune to path-level rename-over races that a separate `os.stat`-before-`open` cannot see), enforces `_MAX_FILE_BYTES` against the pre-read `fstat`, and raises `ResolverInputError` if the two `fstat`s disagree (rewritten in place during the read).
      - `_load_file_lines` pins the fstat-confirmed generation only after a successful read and raises `ResolverInputError` on ANY later open/fstat/read failure or metadata mismatch for an already-pinned path -- both an already-vanished file (`os.open` itself failing) and a mid-run rewrite are covered, not just a metadata-mismatch special case.
      - Design-review-caught bug fixed before shipping: `_read_file_lines` was first written `@lru_cache`d by path alone, which never re-opens a path on a second call -- the mutation-detection contract was dead code, since a cached call can never produce a fresh `meta` to compare against the pin.
      - Fixed by re-fstatting on EVERY call (cheap) and reusing the parsed line tuple only when that fresh fstat is unchanged; caught by this section's own regression fixture before it shipped.
      - **Fixed before shipping (Codex adversarial):** the byte ceiling was checked only against the PRE-read `fstat`, so `fh.read()` (unbounded) could consume far more than `_MAX_FILE_BYTES` before a POST-read fstat ever noticed a concurrent-growth race. The read itself is now capped at `_MAX_FILE_BYTES + 1` characters, raising immediately on overrun rather than after the fact.
- [x] Handle backslash-newline splicing in the resolver lexer -- C splices before lexing, so a string or comment continued across a physical line is mis-lexed
      - ARBITRARY-HOP splice-aware lexing in both engines: `_scan_line`/`_iter_code_chars` (the exact structural walk) carry a "pending half-delimiter" (`/` awaiting `*` or `/`; `*` awaiting `/` while in a block comment) across any run of spliced lines that contribute zero scannable characters.
      - Resolves against the first real character reached -- which may be several lines later -- and yields a deferred `/` at its OWN original physical position if it never resolves into a real delimiter.
      - `_LEX_SPAN_RE` (the regex-based candidate-discovery index) got the matching generalization: `(?:\\\n)*` (zero or more LITERAL backslash-newline pairs, never a bare unspliced newline) between each delimiter's two characters. Both engines verified to agree via a direct `_FileIndex` span check.
      - Codex design review caught two real gaps in the first draft before it shipped: single-hop-only carry (missed a delimiter split across 2+ splices) and a regex draft that would have matched an ordinary unspliced newline as if it were a splice.
      - **FAIL-CLOSED, not just documented (Codex adversarial, 3rd round upgraded this from a documented residual):** a string/char literal whose escaping backslash lands exactly on a spliced line boundary (an EVEN-length trailing backslash run, not just exactly two) cannot be resolved safely by a per-line scanner that does not carry escape state across the splice. `_scan_line` now raises a module-private `_AmbiguousEscapeSplice` the moment it detects this exact shape; all three `_iter_code_chars` callers catch it and degrade to the same "not found" result an ordinary unresolvable input already produces -- never a silently wrong range.
      - A corrected whole-tree, parity-aware scan (every `.c`/`.h` file, any even-length run >= 2) finds exactly ONE hit in the entire repo (`tools/firmware-tables-decode.c:10`), and it sits inside a COMMENT, never live string content -- the original documentation both undercounted the search scope (src/+include/ only) and searched for the wrong backslash count (4, not 2, due to shell-quoting arithmetic).
- [x] Regression fixtures for every shape above, each mutation-checked, plus a corpus run that raises the baseline
      - 28 new fixtures in `scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` (14aa-14mm; 146 -> 173 tests): spliced `/*` open, spliced `*/` close, multi-hop splice (empty splice-only line between delimiter halves), a negative control proving an unspliced newline never opens a comment.
      - Also: the `candidate_lines` per-occurrence regression this section ALSO found and fixed (see Notes), oversized-file `ResolverInputError`, mutation-detected `ResolverInputError`, and a growth-race fixture that SPIES on the exact `read()` call argument (mutation-verified: reverting the bounded read to `fh.read()` makes the fixture fail).
      - Also: `follow_declaration` header-resolves / non-header-stays-unresolved / static-rejected (leading, non-leading, AND on-the-preceding-line) / ambiguous-basename-stays-unresolved, `corpus_resolution_snapshot.py` surfacing `ResolverInputError` as exit 3 rather than an uncaught traceback, and a fixture proving the fail-closed escape-splice degrade (not a silently wrong range).
      - `scripts/lint/stub-lint-baseline.json` raised 57 -> 111 (the `resolved_n` floor); `corpus_resolution_snapshot.py compare` against the pre-section baseline reports 0 lost / 0 moved, 54 added (covering BOTH direct resolution and `follow_declaration`, after the identity-coverage gap below was fixed), every addition hand-verified.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: resolve definitions past the head limit and harden the resolver lexer"`

## 13. Stored-Ref Repair: Unpaired Symbols and Bare Filenames

> **Spawned-by:** §12 (split)

§11's population repair counted the whole corpus for the first time and found that the two largest unresolved classes are not resolver gaps at all: 1,122 unpaired symbol refs (69%) and 246 bare-filename refs (15%) are refs no file-scoped resolver can act on as stored, produced by [`extract_stamped_items`](../../scripts/todo-graph/build.py) and written into `build/todo-cache.json` before any resolver runs. Together they are 84% of the population -- more than five times everything §12 can reach.

**The framing that opened this section was WRONG, and the section's own measurement is what disproved it.** Both classes were filed as BAD STORED DATA to be rewritten in `build.py`, so the section was scoped as a cache change with every cache consumer in its blast radius. Neither half survived contact: a bare filename IS a fact about the source tree, so storing its resolution would create a cache claim that goes stale on a source edit with nothing able to notice (the freshness check compares the cache mtime against `todo/**/*.md` only); and the unpaired class is mostly an authoring convention, not corrupt data, so there was nothing to repair for ~90% of it. **The cache is unchanged by this section.** Both repairs are applied at RESOLUTION time, so `validate.py`, `query.py`, the MCP server and every hook built on them see exactly what they saw before -- the blast radius is the two consumers that read `stamped_items[].refs`, lint Check 7 and the identity snapshot.

The §12 split still earned itself, on the boundary that turned out to matter: §12 owns `resolve_symbol.py`'s definition rule, this section owns which FILE that rule is pointed at. Reviewing both under one gate would have hidden which of the two moved a mapping.

It does NOT re-cover §12's head-limit widening, decl-following, cache bounds, or lexer splicing, nor §11's counting base. The `corpus_resolution_snapshot.py` identity check is the acceptance test here too: every ADDED mapping is ground-truthed by hand before the baseline moves, and 0 lost / 0 moved is the bar. It is a MANUAL, on-demand `write`/`compare` -- §12 deliberately parked its automated wiring after two design rounds found real correctness gaps in every mechanism tried, so nothing runs it for you and calling it a wired gate would be false comfort. §16 owns making it one; §14 owns making it COVER the whole population, which is a different defect and lands first.

**Establish the MEANING before writing code.** Both items were a question about the corpus, not a bug with a known fix, and the answer decided which file changed. Guessing here would have rewritten 1,368 stored refs on an assumption -- and the measurement did in fact move the repair OUT of the extractor this section was named for.

- [x] Decide what an UNPAIRED symbol ref means -- **1,122 occurrences, 69% of the whole population; the single biggest class in the corpus**
      These are `kind=symbol` refs carrying a symbol and NO file (`{"kind":"symbol","symbol":"thread_yield"}`), so there is nothing for a file-scoped resolver to open. `extract_stamped_items` sets `file` only from the FIRST file ref in the SAME item, so an item naming a symbol with no file link produces one by construction.
      - **ANSWER: mostly a CONVENTION, partly a lost pairing -- and NOT a tree-wide resolution problem.** An unpaired ref happens exactly when the item text holds zero file refs, so nothing is being dropped upstream at the item level; the reading that "the item legitimately named a symbol without a file" is the majority case.
      - **Tree-wide pairing MEASURED and then REJECTED.** 673 of the 736 unique unpaired symbols (1,028 occurrences) do have exactly one C definition tree-wide, 4 are ambiguous and 59 have none (macros, python/shell function names, asm labels). Binding on that alone is the mechanism §12 already rejected with reason (a unique-name search can bind an unrelated same-named definition in another link product, with no way to prove linkage), and §12's one narrow use of it was paid for by hand-verifying all 18 mappings. 673 is 37x that and unverifiable at section scale.
      - **Authored scope does not exist in this corpus either:** exactly 1 of 232 TODO nodes carries a `file_patterns` frontmatter list, and that node holds zero unpaired refs. So scoping candidates by declared file patterns cannot recover anything today.
      - **SECTION SCOPE is the authored evidence that does exist, and it recovers 264.** Pairing a symbol to a section file only when EXACTLY ONE of them defines it (resolver-confirmed, never proximity) recovered **106** on the first pass, when only refs whose section named an EXISTING path could contribute candidates. Round 2 admitted bare filenames as section candidates too and it rose to **264**; the residual unpaired population is **858**. Final provenance across the 1,625 occurrences: 193 authored, 238 basename-repaired, 264 section-paired, 858 unpaired, 63 non-C, 8 missing-file, 1 path-escape.
      - The residual 858 stay a COUNTING-contract question, not an extractor bug: an item that names an API in prose with no file anywhere in its section is authored that way on purpose, and `unpaired-ref` continues to report it.
- [x] Resolve BARE-FILENAME refs against the tree -- **246 occurrences / 232 unique, the largest recoverable class among PAIRED refs**
      EVERY missing-file ref is a bare filename with no directory (`boot_desktop.c:boot_run_deferred`), stored verbatim as a repo-relative path that never exists at the repo root -- while the file itself does exist (`src/kernel/main/boot_desktop.c`).
      - MEASURED: of the 232 unique, **224 have a UNIQUE basename in the tree, 7 are ambiguous, 1 is absent** (72 unique FILENAMES behind them: 69 unique-basename, 2 ambiguous `entropy.c`/`entropy.h`, 1 absent `hv_bar.h`). 238 occurrences repaired; the 8 that remain -- 7 across the 2 ambiguous basenames plus 1 for the absent one -- are never guessed and stay counted in `missing-file`.
      - **The repair does NOT belong in extraction, contrary to this item's original plan.** Codex design review, verified at source: `build.py`'s extraction is a pure function of (TODO body, TODO path), and the cache's freshness check compares the cache mtime against `todo/**/*.md` ONLY ([`check_stub_behind_stamp.py`](../../scripts/lint/check_stub_behind_stamp.py) `_check_staleness`). A basename is a fact about the SOURCE TREE, so storing one would create a cache claim that goes stale on a source edit with nothing able to notice it.
      - Both repairs therefore live in a new [`scripts/todo-graph/ref_resolution.py`](../../scripts/todo-graph/ref_resolution.py) applied at RESOLUTION time. The cache keeps saying exactly what the author wrote, so no consumer is handed inferred provenance dressed as authored provenance.
      - **One shared rule, two callers.** `classify_ref` is imported by BOTH lint Check 7's walk and [`corpus_resolution_snapshot.py`](../../scripts/todo-graph/corpus_resolution_snapshot.py)'s `collect()`, because the snapshot skipped every ref without a stored `file` and would otherwise have been blind to exactly the mappings it is supposed to gate -- the same incomplete-proof defect §12's decl-following change shipped once already.
- [x] Regression fixtures for both shapes, each mutation-checked, plus a corpus run that raises the baseline
      Thirteen sub-tests (14j-14v) in [`scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh`](../../scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh), each asserting the EXACT effective path rather than "a finding appeared" -- the whole risk in both repairs is binding to the WRONG file, which a finding-count assertion cannot see. 186/186 green.
      - Negatives are the load-bearing half: ambiguous basename (two `dup.c`), absent basename, ambiguous section scope (two files both defining the symbol), and no cross-section leak.
      - **19 mutations run, every one detected; 12 of the 13 fixtures are flipped by one** (basename repair disabled / uniqueness relaxed to pick-first / section pairing disabled / accessor guard removed / ambiguous section scope guessed / section scope widened to the whole node / identity gate reverted to skipping unstored files / section candidates drop bare file refs / narrow accessor regex / statement-keyword guard removed / comparison lookarounds removed / shared cache_clear removed). 14l (absent basename) is labelled IN-FILE as a behavior pin: it asserts an absence that both correct and plausibly-broken code produce, so no single mutation flips it.
      - **Coverage 111 -> 474 (+363), a 4.3x rise**, proved at **0 lost / 0 moved** by `corpus_resolution_snapshot.py`. All 363 added mappings ground-truthed: 0 failed an independent head/close-brace check, 0 targeted a non-tree-unique basename, and 22 stratified spot-checks (statics, header inlines, bootloader, kernel, drivers, tests) read exact at file:line.
      - One false positive surfaced by the new visibility was fixed at its CAUSE, not marked: `return <bare identifier>;` is no longer a stub when the identifier names a file-scope VARIABLE in the same translation unit, because that is an accessor reading state (`compositor_get_test_seed()`, [`src/kernel/main/compositor.c`](../../src/kernel/main/compositor.c):64). `return STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED;` still fires -- the rule the check exists for is untouched.
      - The one genuine stub found (`test_usermode_color_active`, the release-flavor no-op inline) got the existing `INTENTIONAL-STUB` marker, alongside its sibling `test_usermode_capture_byte` which is the same construct.
- [x] Round 2 (Codex adversarial + consistency + perf, 5 findings): 4 fixed, 1 filed as §14
      - **[high] The basename repair was applied to symbol refs but NOT to the file refs that build the section candidate list**, so `section_candidate_files` silently dropped every bare file ref and starved the pairing of exactly the sections most likely to need it. MEASURED: 673 of 1,167 stamped `.c`/`.h` file refs do not exist at their authored path, 629 bare and tree-unique. One `_effective_path` helper now serves both ref kinds, deduped by canonical path: **+158 occurrences, 316 -> 474**.
      - **[medium] The accessor exemption's first regex recognized only `TYPE name = | ; | [`**, so a getter for `static int a, name;`, `static int (*name)(void);`, `_Atomic(int) name;` or `static int name __attribute__((unused));` was still flagged -- failing toward BLOCKING the lint on legitimate code. Widened to those declarator shapes with both directions pinned: a `#define`, an enum constant, an indented local and a parameter name still do not satisfy it.
      - **[medium] Perf: `_defining_candidates` re-resolved per (section file, symbol) with no memo, and the caller resolved the winner a second time.** Fixed with a raw-substring pre-filter (raw text is a superset of code text, so it can only skip a file that provably does not name the symbol -- never change a verdict), a shared resolve memo Check 7's own cache now delegates to, and a memoized `_effective_path` (the `Path.resolve()`/`relative_to()` pair was 5,146 calls and ~1.1s of a 3.7s profile). Measured over 5 fresh-process runs: **Check 7 0.46s -> 1.34s** for 4.3x the coverage; profiled work fell 3.7s -> 1.5s. Codex's batched per-section definition index was NOT taken: it is a SECOND definition-finding implementation, which is the exact divergence this module exists to prevent.
      - **[medium] The section preamble still described the rejected cache-mutation design** after the items had been rewritten. Corrected in place, including the reciprocal XREFs, and the false "BAD STORED DATA" framing is now recorded as disproven rather than deleted.
      - **[high] Filed, not fixed: the identity gate still records only RESOLVED mappings** (695 snapshot entries vs 1,625 occurrences), so a ref moving between unresolved buckets is invisible to it. Real, but it changes the gate's baseline value format and its `resolved` semantics, which is its own review surface -> §14.
- [x] Round 3 (Codex adversarial + consistency + perf, 6 findings): all 6 resolved, 2 of them defects the round-2 fixes had just introduced
      - **[high] FAIL-OPEN, introduced by round 2's own widening: a `_Static_assert` about a constant suppressed the stub that returns it.** Admitting parentheses to the declarator prefix plus accepting the first `=` of `==` made `_Static_assert(STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED == -1, "contract");` read as a DECLARATION of that constant, so `return STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED;` stopped being a finding -- the exact class this lint exists for, in the one direction that is never acceptable. Reproduced end-to-end before fixing. Closed by TWO independent guards, each separately mutation-verified: comparison lookarounds so `==`/`!=`/`<=`/`>=` cannot read as an initializer, and a line-leading keyword reject for `_Static_assert`/`typedef`/`return`/`if`/... Word boundaries were added around the declarator in the same pass so `s_v` no longer matches inside `other_s_v`.
      - **[medium] The delegated resolve memo broke an existing test contract and left the two caches resettable only separately.** `_resolve_cached` was an `lru_cache` and [`scripts/overnight/tests/test_stub_lint_coverage.py`](../../scripts/overnight/tests/test_stub_lint_coverage.py) resets all four caches through `.cache_clear`; delegating dropped the attribute and raised `AttributeError`. That file is run-owned control plane, so the repair is on this side: the attribute is preserved explicitly and routed at the memo that now holds the entries, and `clear_caches()` also calls `resolve_symbol.cache_clear()` so answers and the content they were derived from can never be reset independently.
      - **[medium] Perf, the growth shape rather than the constant.** `_mentions` scanned every candidate file per symbol, keeping the pairing proportional to `unpaired symbols x section files x FILE SIZE`, unbounded in the first two. Replaced by a per-file cached identifier SET, so the pre-filter is O(1) per question and the tokenization is paid once per file and reused across every section naming it. `_defining_candidates` fell to 0.96s cumulative of which 0.92s is `resolve_symbol` itself on files that genuinely mention the symbol -- irreducible without a second definition rule. Codex's per-section batched index was again declined for that reason; the sound half of its advice (batch the PRE-FILTER, not the definition rule) is what shipped.
      - **[medium] §14's own population contract was wrong in the draft filed above** -- it claimed 930 omitted while listing buckets summing to 1,019, put `no-calllike-token` among the omitted when it is stored as a null, and asserted `classify_ref` already supplies every bucket. It does not: it returns `bucket=None` for a valid-path unresolved ref, and the two buckets in §14's motivating example are assigned by the walk AFTER resolution fails. Corrected, including what §14 must therefore actually move.
      - **[medium] Two stale cross-references survived the round-2 prose fix:** §12's handoff paragraph still sent this section's work into `build.py` extraction, and §11 had no reciprocal XREF to the new §14. Both repaired.
- [x] Round 4, POST-COMMIT (Codex adversarial + consistency + perf, 7 findings): all 7 fixed, 3 of them fail-opens
      - **[high] The recorded DENOMINATOR was written and never read.** `stub-lint-baseline.json` carries `total`, but `_read_baseline()` only returned `resolved`, so a cache that stopped emitting UNRESOLVED refs shrank the buckets and the population together, left `resolved` at its floor, published a BETTER ratio and exited 0 -- fail-open on exactly the corpus rot §11's counted population exists to catch. Now enforced: any move in either direction is rc 7. Proved by rotting a copy of the live cache (unpaired refs stripped): 1,625 -> 503 occurrences, rc 7 with a POPULATION SHRANK message.
      - **[high] File scope was inferred from COLUMN 0, which is not evidence of it.** An unindented struct member (`int NAME;` inside a struct body) or a multi-line enumerator line (`OK, NAME,`) read as a file-scope object and suppressed a genuine `return NAME;` stub. Both are valid C this tree could adopt tomorrow. Replaced with a real brace-depth-0 test over the masked whole-file text, which also subsumes the function-local case.
      - **[medium] The resolution memo answered without re-entering the drift check.** `_load_file_lines` is the only layer that re-stats a pinned file and raises `ResolverInputError`; a memo hit skipped it, so a repeated `(file, symbol)` after a mid-run rewrite got pre-mutation coordinates instead of the promised rc 9. Both caches now call it on every probe and bind their entry to the returned lines OBJECT.
      - **[medium] Perf, a defect this section's own round-3 fix introduced:** keying the mention index on the line TUPLE made Python re-hash every line on each lookup, so the "O(1)" pre-filter was linear in file length (measured 0.680s vs 0.0023s over 20,000 probes against `bootx64.c`). Re-keyed on the path with an identity check against the lines object -- constant-time AND content-bound.
      - **[medium] Perf: candidate paths were re-canonicalized per probe, and built for all 830 sections when only 357 hold an unpaired symbol.** `section_candidate_files` now returns already-validated ABSOLUTE paths consumed directly, and is built lazily. Check 7 median over 5 fresh processes: **0.43s at HEAD -> 1.23s** (was 1.54s before these two), for 4.3x the coverage.
      - **[medium] §13 called the identity snapshot a gate that §12 "wires".** §12 explicitly PARKED the automated wiring after two design rounds found correctness gaps in every mechanism tried, so nothing runs it automatically. Reworded to manual/on-demand; §14 owns making it a real gate. Calling it wired was false comfort about the protection future resolver work has.
      - **[low] The baseline said "7 ambiguous basenames"** when it is 7 occurrences across 2 ambiguous basenames plus 1 for an absent one. Corrected in both the baseline and the section body.

The fix loop is where this section's real risk lived, not the original design.

## 14. Identity Gate Over the Whole Symbol-Ref Population

> **Spawned-by:** §13 (review)

[`corpus_resolution_snapshot.py`](../../scripts/todo-graph/corpus_resolution_snapshot.py) is the gate that proves a resolver change added mappings without losing or moving any, and every section since §11 has been accepted on its `0 lost / 0 moved` verdict. It records a verdict for only part of the population, in two distinct ways. `collect()` returns 695 entries against Check 7's 1,625 `kind=symbol` occurrences: 474 resolution tuples plus 221 nulls. The other **930 have no entry at all** -- `unpaired-ref` 858, `missing-file` 8, non-c-suffix 63, path-escape 1 -- because a ref that never reaches resolution is filtered out before a key is minted. And the 221 nulls are recorded only as "did not resolve", with no record of WHICH bucket: `calllike-unresolved` (132) and `no-calllike-token` (89) are indistinguishable in the snapshot.

That is a real hole in the proof, and §13 is the section that demonstrates it. §13 moved refs BETWEEN unresolved buckets at scale (`calllike-unresolved` 35 -> 132, `no-calllike-token` 47 -> 89) as repaired refs entered the population that gets looked at. Nothing gated those movements: the `resolved` floor sees the total rise, and the bucket line is printed but not compared, so a change that quietly reclassified hundreds of refs the other way would read as clean.

- [x] Record a verdict for EVERY `kind=symbol` occurrence -- `collect()` covers 1,626, was 695
      `collect()` no longer skips a ref that never reaches resolution, and a ref that reaches it and fails now records WHICH bucket instead of a bare null. Value is `[path, start, end]` or a bucket string; the population equals Check 7's by construction (both 1,626 / 474 resolved, and all six bucket counts equal -- asserted by 14aa against Check 7's own printed line, not against a number written in the test).
      - **`classify_ref` alone was NOT sufficient**, exactly as the item predicted: it supplies only the PRE-resolution buckets. The whole end-to-end rule is now `ref_resolution.resolve_ref` (classify -> direct resolve -> `follow_declaration` -> call-like bucket), and `_has_calllike_token`/`_raw_text`/`_code_text`/`_STRIP_RE` moved out of `check_stub_behind_stamp.py` into it.
      - Sharing only the PRE-resolution half was itself the trap, caught by design review: each caller still ran its OWN resolve-then-follow sequence, so a fallback taught to one and not the other recreates the incomplete proof this module exists to prevent. Neither caller now names `follow_declaration` or `classify_ref` -- pinned by 14ee.
      - The key carries the AUTHORED spelling with a `-` sentinel, NOT the effective path. Byte-identity with a schema-1 baseline was the item's stated constraint and it is worth nothing: `compare` refuses any baseline whose schema differs, so an old one can never be compared against these keys anyway. A resolver-output key also changed identity when a repaired ref degraded, reporting DROPPED+ADDED instead of LOST.
- [x] `compare` distinguishes reclassification from loss, and FAILS on it
      Six outcomes: DROPPED (occurrence gone), LOST (resolved -> bucket), MOVED (different coordinates), CHANGED (bucket -> different bucket), GAINED (bucket -> resolved), ADDED. The first four exit 1; GAINED and ADDED exit 0 because neither can hide a loss.
      - **CHANGED FAILS, reversing this item's original stance** on design-review evidence. The item said "not a second blocking threshold", but that guards against a numeric floor; this is identity comparison, which already blocks. The exit code is the only part a wired gate can read (§16), so reporting a reclassification while exiting 0 would let a defect that moved hundreds of refs ship unattended. Clearing path is the roadmap's existing one: ground-truth by hand, then regenerate.
      - The `resolved` floor in [`stub-lint-baseline.json`](../../scripts/lint/stub-lint-baseline.json) is unchanged and still the coverage gate.
- [x] Baseline format migration, fail-closed on an old baseline
      `SNAPSHOT_SCHEMA` 1 -> 2; a schema-1 baseline is refused rc 3 with a regenerate pointer. Bucket strings are validated against the exact `ALL_BUCKETS` enum, so a typo'd bucket is a hard error rather than a permanent CHANGED nothing can clear.
      - Migration proved by hand across the schema boundary rather than asserted: a schema-1 snapshot taken from the pre-change code, correlated to the schema-2 snapshot by occurrence coordinate, gives **0 lost / 0 moved / 0 gained over all 474 resolved mappings**, and all 221 former nulls now carry a real bucket.
      - Also fail-closed on its two INPUT shapes, both found by adversarial review: an invalid-UTF-8 baseline raised `UnicodeDecodeError` (a `ValueError`, so uncaught) and escaped as exit 1 -- colliding with exit 1's documented "a prior verdict changed"; and a mixed cache passed the `any(...)` presence test, then raised `AttributeError` from inside the walk with the same collision. Both are rc 3 now, with every node/item/ref shape rejected up front.
- [x] Regression fixtures, each mutation-checked -- 14aa-14gg plus 13 refused-input cases
      [`scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh`](../../scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh) 204/204. Ten mutations applied and each caught by a named test: population narrowed, CHANGED made non-failing, schema check disabled, bucket enum removed, `@lru_cache` restored on the text helpers, per-file strip disabled, a caller calling `follow_declaration` again, the sibling binding removed, `ValueError` no longer caught, node-shape validation removed.
      - **Two mutations initially SURVIVED and the tests were strengthened rather than the result recorded**: the first sibling-binding mutation was unfaithful (it kept the `_load_file_lines` call the original `@lru_cache` defect skipped) and the first orchestration mutation referenced `follow_declaration` without calling it. Re-run faithfully, both fail a named test.
      - The fixture corpus gained two refs that REACH resolution and fail there, because it previously produced no post-resolution buckets at all -- so a CHANGED assertion could not exercise `unresolved_calllike` vs `no_calllike_token`, the pair §13 actually moved refs between.
      - Six baseline fixtures were passing for the WRONG REASON: they still declared schema 1, so `compare` returned at the schema mismatch and never reached the element validation they were written to test. Deleting arity, path-type, range or truncation validation would have left them green. All are schema 2 now, plus cases for a two-element mapping, a non-string and empty path, a bool in either line position, and a zero start line.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: identity gate covers every symbol ref, not only resolved ones"`

**Test checkpoint:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` 204/204; `corpus_resolution_snapshot.py compare` clean against a regenerated baseline and refusing an old-schema one (rc 3); `bash scripts/lint.sh` Check 7 at `resolved=474/1626` with `resolved` and five of six buckets unmoved (`unpaired-ref` 858 -> 859: stamping this section's own items added one symbol ref no file in its section defines, re-recorded deliberately after the gate refused with rc 7); `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` 1287/1287; `bash scripts/overnight/tests/run-all.sh` 66/66.

## 15. The Shared Rule's Cost: Structural-Walk Hot Path and Split Cache Lifecycles

> **Spawned-by:** §14 (split)

Split out of §14 at authoring time, not spawned by a review round. §14 proves the identity gate covers every `kind=symbol` ref; this section is about what that shared rule COSTS to evaluate. The two failure modes are opposite and are verified by different means: §14 fails by mis-verdicting a ref and is proven by functional fixtures, while this one fails by scaling badly or retaining memory and can only be proven by a SCALING fixture. Bundling them is how a wall-clock number gets accepted as a growth-shape proof.

- [x] Cut the shared rule's cost at the hot path the MEASUREMENT found -- the batched-lookup premise this item was filed on did not survive profiling
      The item assumed per-symbol candidate rescanning was the cost. It is not: `candidate_lines` is **0.071s of a ~1.5s walk (~5%)**, because `str.find` runs at C speed -- the "65 MB of repeated scanning" is real in bytes and ~70ms in time. The ~1.0s under `_defining_candidates` is definition CONFIRMATION, not discovery.
      - The real hot path was [`_iter_code_chars`](../../scripts/todo-graph/resolve_symbol.py): **1,308,110 characters yielded and 2.19M list appends in one walk (0.649s)**, so that three brace counters could test each character against `{` and `}`. Fixed by giving `_scan_line`/`_iter_code_chars` a `wanted` output filter and passing `_BRACES` / `_PARENS_BRACES` at the three body-walk call sites. The lexer state machine is UNCHANGED -- comment, literal and splice handling still see every character -- so what counts as code cannot shift; only materialization narrows.
      - `_confirm_definition` is deliberately NOT filtered: it inspects every character (`isspace` / `isalnum` / a catch-all reject branch), so narrowing it would change what it rejects. That is why `wanted` is a per-caller argument rather than a global.
      - MEASURED after: **5.09M -> 2.54M function calls, 1.67M -> 0.40M list appends, 1263ms -> 1177ms median (6.8%)** over 7 interleaved runs against the pre-change tree, with `corpus_resolution_snapshot.py compare` reporting 0 dropped / 0 lost / 0 moved / 0 reclassified across all 1,626 refs. The call-count drop is the durable number; the wall-clock ratio held at 6-7% across separately-loaded hosts.
      - BATCHING WAS BUILT, MEASURED AND REMOVED -- recorded so it is not re-litigated. A `resolve_symbols(file, symbol_set)` entry point plus `candidate_lines_batch` was implemented behind the same `_resolve_from_candidates` rule and verified verdict-identical (720 real resolutions, 0 mismatches). It made Check 7 **SLOWER**: a tokenize-and-bucket pass costs more in Python than the C-speed `str.find` it replaces (2.9s), and a `\b`-anchored alternation still cost 2.1s against HEAD's 1.5s. Section symbol sets are large enough that the alternation itself dominates.
      - PRECISELY WHAT THAT DOES AND DOES NOT SETTLE. It refutes batching as a WIN AT THIS CORPUS'S SCALE, and it refutes the cost ATTRIBUTION the item was filed on. It does NOT refute the growth shape, which is real and still present -- a corpus with far more symbols per section, or far larger files, moves the crossover. The honest close is the measured limitation plus 15c's shape fixture, not a claim that the question is closed forever (Codex perf, section 15).
      - Sequenced AFTER §14 as planned: §14's whole-population identity gate is what made a rewrite of the shared rule's hot path provable rather than plausible.
- [x] Bound the process-wide caches by WHAT PINS THEM, which splits them into two lifecycles rather than one bound
      The item assumed one policy for all four dicts. A Codex design round showed that is unsafe: `_BASENAME_INDEX` and `_EFFECTIVE_PATH_MEMO` answer from directory TOPOLOGY (which files exist, whether a basename is unique) and nothing pins that, so an LRU eviction mid-walk can recompute against a changed tree and give a late ref a different verdict than an early one -- the exact silent RESULT change this item forbids.
      - Those two are therefore WALK-SCOPED: unbounded inside one walk so every ref sees one topology snapshot, released at the boundary by `clear_caches()` or the new `walk_scope()` context manager. Memory is bounded by the walk rather than by a capacity that would have to trade consistency for it.
      - `_RESOLVE_MEMO`, `_MENTION_INDEX`, `_RAW_TEXT` and `_CODE_TEXT` store `(lines object, value)` and revalidate by identity on every read, so an eviction can only cost a recompute of the same answer. Those are LRU-bounded (`_Lru`, capacities 512/8192 sized against the measured ~924 / ~998 / ~260 working set, so an ordinary walk never evicts).
      - `_END_TO_END` LOOKS content-bound and is not: a miss or a followed-header answer also depends on `follow_declaration`'s live `src/**/<basename>.c` glob, which no lines object pins. It was bounded in the first draft and the adversarial round caught it -- creating a sibling can turn zero matches into one without invalidating any entry, which is only tolerable while the whole walk shares one snapshot. Walk-scoped with the other two.
      - Both gates now ENTER the lifecycle (`with _rr.walk_scope():` around `_walk` and `collect`). The first draft shipped the context manager as an API no production caller used, so the release-at-boundary property it advertised was not actually realized -- also an adversarial finding.
      - The "one topology snapshot" claim was made TRUE rather than narrowed: `follow_declaration` globbed `src/**/<basename>.c` live on every call, so two refs in ONE walk could straddle a file appearing. New walk-scoped `_sibling_candidates()` freezes it per (root, basename), putting declaration-following on the same footing as `_basename_index`.
      - `walk_scope()` is RE-ENTRANT and restores a saved depth in a `finally` that opens before the increment. Both halves were review findings: a nested exit would otherwise drop `resolve_symbol`'s generation pins mid-walk (turning a documented rc 9 drift failure into a silent clean read), and an exception in the increment window would strand the depth and disable clearing for the process. The module is single-threaded BY CONTRACT -- a lock here was rejected, because every cache it and `resolve_symbol` hold is unsynchronized process-global state and locking one boundary would advertise safety the module lacks.
      - The multiplicative shape is NOT removed and is now pinned rather than argued away: `_defining_candidates` remains O(symbols x candidate files x file bytes). Fixture 15c measures each axis independently in operation COUNTS (doubling symbols or files doubles scans; growing file size grows scanned bytes at a constant scan count), so a future regression is caught by a shape test rather than a wall-clock number on one corpus.
      - Still housekeeping, not a correctness gate, exactly as filed -- each lint invocation is a fresh interpreter. The value is that an in-process importer now has a boundary at all.
- [x] Commit: `"todo-graph: cut the shared rule's hot path and split the cache lifecycles"`

**Test checkpoint:** MET. `test_build.sh` 213/213 -- 15a (body-walk shape), 15b (cache lifecycles), 15c (pairing growth on all three axes), 15d (re-entrancy + frozen sibling glob), every one mutation-checked; `corpus_resolution_snapshot.py compare` clean against §14's baseline (0 dropped / lost / moved / reclassified over 1,626 refs); Check 7 `resolved=474/1626` unchanged; `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` 1287/1287.

> **Test runner:** `bash scripts/todo-graph/tests/test_build.sh` -- expect `213/213 passed, 0 failed`. Also `bash scripts/test-tooling.sh` (1287/1287) and `bash scripts/lint.sh` reporting Check 7 `resolved=474/1626` with no ERROR lines.

-> XREF: [`TODO-06 §13`](#13-stored-ref-repair-unpaired-symbols-and-bare-filenames) -- whose round-3 perf review measured the per-symbol scanning this section profiled and found to be ~5% of the walk, not its cost.

> **Notes:**
> - Shipped a `wanted` output filter on `_scan_line`/`_iter_code_chars` plus `_BRACES`/`_PARENS_BRACES` at the three body-walk call sites; the lexer state machine is untouched, so only materialization narrows.
> - Split `ref_resolution`'s caches by what pins them: topology-derived ones walk-scoped behind `walk_scope()`, content-bound ones LRU-bounded via `_Lru`.
> - Added `SectionScope`/`section_scope()` as the ONE shared per-section constructor, replacing the `_needs` + `section_candidate_files` preamble both callers had copied.
> - Measured: 5.09M -> 2.54M calls and 1263ms -> 1177ms median (6.8%), verdict-identical across all 1,626 refs by the §14 identity gate; Codex independently reproduced ~7.5%.
> - The filed premise (per-symbol rescanning is the cost) was refuted by profiling; the batched entry point was built, measured slower, and removed rather than shipped to satisfy the wording. The multiplicative growth SHAPE remains and is pinned by fixture 15c rather than argued away.
> - Scope boundary: does NOT wire the identity gate into anything (§16 owns that) and does not change any resolution verdict.
